\textbf{Component} & \textbf{Cost} & \textbf{Notes} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Component} & \textbf{Cost} & \textbf{Notes} \\
\midrule
\endhead
N5 front-end + BEOL & \$14,000 & Standard N5 wafer cost \\
FeFET module (11 masks) & \$2,500 & +18\% to standard \\
\midrule
\textbf{Total wafer cost} & \textbf{\$16,500} & \textbf{300~mm wafer} \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

\section{Yield Model}

The die yield is computed using the Murphy model with a defect density $D_0$ per cm$^2$ of die area.

\begin{equation}
Y_{\rm die} = \left( \frac{1 - e^{-A \cdot D_0}}{A \cdot D_0} \right)^2
\end{equation}

\subsection{Mature yield ($D_0$ = 0.1/cm$^2$)}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{2.5cm}p{2cm}p{2cm}p{2cm}p{2.5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Die} & \textbf{Area} & \textbf{Yield} & \textbf{Dies/wafer} & \textbf{Cost per good die} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Die} & \textbf{Area} & \textbf{Yield} & \textbf{Dies/wafer} & \textbf{Cost per good die} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Phone & 150~mm$^2$ & 86.2\% & 407 gross, 350 good & \$47.01 \\
PC & 292~mm$^2$ & 75.1\% & 209 gross, 157 good & \$105.10 \\
DC & 205.9~mm$^2$ & 81.7\% & 296 gross, 242 good & \$68.18 \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

\subsection{First-year yield ($D_0$ = 0.3/cm$^2$)}

First-year yield (defect density typically drops from 0.3 to 0.1 as the process matures over 12--18 months) is significantly lower. First-year pricing reflects this; the mature pricing in Part 13 assumes the mature yield.

\section{Foundry Requirements}

The hybrid N5 + FeFET MEOL flow requires a foundry that offers both N5 CMOS and FeFET MEOL. The physical requirements are N5 CMOS capacity (any foundry currently producing N5 or N4/N3 with porting), MEOL insertion capability (500~°C spike anneal between M3 and M4, standard for embedded NVM), and the FeFET-specific process module (HZO ALD + RTA, developed in cooperation with the foundry).

The FeFET cell is a commercial device (GlobalFoundries 22FDX ships HZO FeFET non-volatile memory today; TSMC has FeFET in research). The N5 FeFET module is a port of the 22FDX process module to N5, using the same HZO material and the same RTA anneal.

\section{Fabrication Acceptance Criteria}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{4cm}p{3.5cm}p{4cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Parameter} & \textbf{Acceptance} & \textbf{Measurement} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Parameter} & \textbf{Acceptance} & \textbf{Measurement} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Orthorhombic phase fraction & $\ge$ 70\% & GI-XRD per wafer \\
Memory window at 67~°C & $\ge$ 2.5~V & 32 cells/wafer \\
Coercive field at 67~°C & $\le$ 1.35~MV/cm & 32 cells/wafer \\
Programmed $\sigma$ (1$\sigma$) & $\le$ 0.10~$\mu$A & 32 cells/wafer \\
Endurance at $\tau \leq 25$~ns & $\ge$ $10^7$ cycles & Write-cycling \\
Reflow loss & $<$ 2\% & 260~°C, 150~s, 3 cycles \\
Retention (woken, 67~°C) & $\ge$ 10 years & 1000~h bake at 125~°C extrapolated \\
Column leakage (2$\sigma$) & $\le$ 406~nA & Full-die leakage test \\
Read access time & $\le$ 120 cycles & Timing test \\
Write row time & $\le$ 100~ns & Timing test \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

\section{Fabrication Risk Summary}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{4cm}p{2.5cm}p{4cm}p{3cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Risk} & \textbf{Likelihood} & \textbf{Mitigation} & \textbf{Fallback} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Risk} & \textbf{Likelihood} & \textbf{Mitigation} & \textbf{Fallback} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Orthorhombic phase $<$ 70\% & Medium & RTA tune 500--520~°C & Reduce HZO thickness to 8~nm \\
Interface trap density high & Medium & SiO$_2$ anneal optimization & Reduce SiO$_2$ to 1.2~nm \\
Reflow loss $>$ 2\% & Medium & Pre-wake-up adjustments & Increase pre-wake-up pulses \\
500~°C damage to M0--M3 & Low & Spike-anneal profile & Reduce RTA to 480~°C (drops phase) \\
Cu diffusion through HZO & Low & Standard TiN barrier & Standard fix \\
Endurance $<$ $10^7$ & Medium & Reduce $\tau$ to 10~ns & Reduce write budget from 215 to 54 writes/cell \\
MEOL integration yield & Medium & Standard NVM flow & Standard foundry co-development \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

None of these is a physics risk. Each has a defined mitigation and a defined fallback.

%=====================================================================
\part{Verification and Qualification}
%=====================================================================

This part specifies the verification and qualification gates. There are twelve gates: Gates 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 5b, 5c, 6, 7, 8, 9, and 10. Each gate has a defined acceptance criterion, a measurement method, and a pass/fail disposition.

\section{Gate Structure Overview}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.5cm}p{4cm}p{2cm}p{3cm}p{3cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Gate} & \textbf{What} & \textbf{Where} & \textbf{What it tests} & \textbf{Disposition} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Gate} & \textbf{What} & \textbf{Where} & \textbf{What it tests} & \textbf{Disposition} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Gate 1 & FeFET phase & Wafer & Orthorhombic phase fraction & Scrap if $<$ 70\% \\
Gate 2 & Single cell & Wafer & Memory window, coercive field, endurance & Scrap or fallback \\
Gate 3 & Small array & Wafer & Array yield, variation & Adjust lot or scrap \\
Gate 4 & Differential pair & Wafer & CMRR, clock skew & Adjust PLL trim \\
Gate 5 & Full die & Packaged & All spec checks & Bin or scrap \\
Gate 5b & Full memory & Packaged & Retention, reflow, disturb & Bin or scrap \\
Gate 5c & DAC/TIA/ADC & Packaged & ENOB, INL, DNL & Bin or scrap \\
Gate 6 & Package/board & Board & BER, IR drop, EMI & Repass or scrap \\
Gate 7 & CPU core & Packaged & ISA compliance, IPC & Bin or reject \\
Gate 8 & Memory hierarchy & Packaged & Latency, bandwidth, coherence & Bin or reject \\
Gate 9 & Multi-socket CXL & Board & Multi-socket scaling & Board respin \\
Gate 10 & Windows-via-Wine & Board & Application compatibility & Documentation \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

Gates 1--4 are sample-based (per wafer, not per die). Gates 5--10 are per-die or per-board.

\section{Gate 1: Orthorhombic Phase}

\textbf{Measurement.} Grazing-incidence X-ray diffraction (GI-XRD) on every wafer. The (111) orthorhombic peak intensity is compared to the (111) monoclinic peak intensity; the ratio is the phase fraction.

\textbf{Acceptance.} $\ge$ 70\% orthorhombic.

\textbf{Disposition.} Wafer below 70\% is scrapped. Wafer 70--80\% is used but flagged for Gate-2 tightened screening. Wafer above 80\% is normal.

\section{Gate 2: Single Cell}

\textbf{Measurement.} 32 cells per wafer, spread across 5 sites. Each cell is programmed, erased, and read using the standard read/write sequences.

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{4cm}p{3cm}p{5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Parameter} & \textbf{Acceptance} & \textbf{Measurement} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Parameter} & \textbf{Acceptance} & \textbf{Measurement} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Memory window at 67~°C & $\ge$ 2.5~V & Programmed $-$ erased threshold \\
Coercive field at 67~°C & $\le$ 1.35~MV/cm & Hysteresis loop midpoint \\
Programmed $\sigma$ (1$\sigma$) & $\le$ 0.10~$\mu$A & Standard deviation of 32 cells \\
Endurance at $\tau \leq 25$~ns & $\ge$ $10^7$ cycles & Write-cycling (accelerated) \\
Reflow loss & $<$ 2\% & 260~°C, 150~s, 3 cycles \\
Retention (woken, 67~°C) & $\ge$ 10 years & 1000~h bake at 125~°C extrapolated \\
Read disturb over $10^{10}$ reads at 85~°C & $<$ 2\% & Stress test \\
Half-select margin & $\ge$ 35\% & Array write test \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

\textbf{Disposition.} Wafer that fails memory window, coercive field, or programmed $\sigma$ is scrapped. Wafer that fails endurance triggers the $\tau \leq$ 10~ns fallback. If memory window is below 2.5~V, the $\pm$1.0~V threshold fallback applies (read current drops to 1.2~$\mu$A, reference retuned to 0.54~$\mu$A).

\section{Gate 3: Small Array}

\textbf{Measurement.} 5 arrays per wafer, each 64$\times$64. Programmed with a test pattern, read back, compared.

\textbf{Acceptance.} Array yield $\ge$ 80\%; cell-to-cell variation $<$ 20\% (1$\sigma$); differential offset $<$ 0.5~mV.

\section{Gate 4: Differential Pair and Clocking}

\textbf{Measurement.} 5 differential pairs per wafer, 5 clock tree test structures per wafer.

\textbf{Acceptance.} CMRR $\ge$ 60~dB at 500~MHz; H-tree skew $\le$ 5~ps; compute PLL jitter $\le$ 1.0~ps RMS; CXL LC-PLL jitter $\le$ 0.6~ps RMS.

\section{Gate 5: Full Die}

\textbf{Measurement.} Automatic test equipment (ATE) per die.

\textbf{Acceptance criteria:} cell SNR $>$ 5$\times$ (25~°C and 85~°C); matching SNR $>$ 5$\times$; thermal (40~°C ambient) T$_j$ $<$ 85~°C; power within spec; functional compute; column leak screening ($\le$ 250~nA normal, 250--400~nA reduced margin, $>$ 400~nA bad); NVL3 factory program verify (3-pass write, $\le$ 1\% columns bad); crossbar factory program verify (Ph-1 and Pc-1 only, 3-pass, $\ge$ 99.99\% cells correct); pre-wake-up applied ($10^4$ crossbar, $10^3$ NVL3); retention post-wake $\ge$ 10 years (crossbar), $\ge$ 8 years (NVL3); boot time from POR $<$ 1.5~ms; host interface functional; JTAG compliance; brown-out response; power-good assertion.

\textbf{Disposition.} Die that passes all criteria is binned to its target product. Die that fails one or more criteria is binned to a lower variant or scrapped.

\section{Gate 5b: Full Memory}

\textbf{Measurement.} Full-array test per die.

\textbf{Acceptance.} All cells programmable and readable; retention 1000~h bake at 125~°C extrapolated $\ge$ 10 years at 67~°C (woken); reflow loss $<$ 2\% after 260~°C/150~s/3 cycles; cross-row disturb: no bit flips in unselected rows during $10^4$ write cycles; row erase-then-program correct; block erase correct.

\section{Gate 5c: DAC / TIA / ADC}

\textbf{Measurement.} Per-die analog test (Ph-1 and Pc-1 only).

\textbf{Acceptance.} DAC monotonicity (16 codes, all monotonic); DAC INL/DNL $\le$ 0.2~mV; TIA transimpedance 2.6--10~k$\Omega$; TIA bandwidth $\ge$ 130~MHz; TIA offset $<$ 1\% full scale; ADC ENOB $\ge$ 8.5 bits (spec target 8.9); ADC INL/DNL within ADC spec; ADC SNR $\ge$ 50~dB.

\section{Gate 6: Package and Board}

\textbf{Measurement.} Per-board test.

\textbf{Acceptance.} Host link BER $<$ $10^{-12}$; board IR drop $<$ 30~mV (VDD\_CORE at maximum load); board thermal within spec at rated cooling; EMI within FCC/CISPR Class B limits; boundary scan (JTAG) all pins accessible; power sequencing correct; power-good assertion.

\section{Gate 7: CPU Core Silicon}

\textbf{Measurement.} Sample 10 dies per lot, standard RISC-V compliance suite and microbenchmarks.

\textbf{Acceptance.} Clock $\ge$ rated $\pm$ 5\%; RV64GCVH instruction coverage 100\% of RV64GC + V + H; MMU Sv39 page walk; hypervisor H extension trap-and-emulate; RVV 512-bit FP32 dot product at rated FLOPS; branch predictor accuracy $\ge$ 96\% on SPECint subset; IPC $\ge$ 4.2 (10-wide) / $\ge$ 4.5 (12-wide) on compute-bound; interrupt latency 6--22 cycles median 12.

\section{Gate 8: Memory Hierarchy Silicon}

\textbf{Measurement.} Sample 10 dies per lot, memory stress and timing benchmarks.

\textbf{Acceptance.} L1 access 4 cycles; L2 access 12 cycles; SRAM L3 access 40 cycles; FeFET NVL3 read 120 cycles; FeFET NVL3 write 100~ns (row write); DDR5 access 250--350 cycles; DDR5 bandwidth $\ge$ 400~GB/s (Pc-1, 8 channels) / $\ge$ 200~GB/s (Dc-1S, 4 channels); MESI coherence 100\% correct; NVL3 retention $\ge$ 10 years (bake extrapolation); NVL3 endurance $\ge$ $10^7$ cycles; NVL3 non-volatility (state retained across power cycle); cache warm-up $\le$ 5~ms.

\section{Gate 9: Multi-Socket CXL}

\textbf{Measurement.} Sample 5 boards per lot, 2-socket and 4-socket configurations.

\textbf{Acceptance.} CXL 3.0 enumeration both sockets visible; CXL.io link up x16 at 64 GT/s; CXL.cache coherence correct; CXL.mem bandwidth $\ge$ 100~GB/s per direction; NUMA topology exposed via ACPI SRAT; boot (2-socket) $\le$ 15~ms to kernel entry; boot (4-socket) $\le$ 30~ms; MT scaling (2-socket) $\ge$ 1.8$\times$ single-socket; (4-socket) $\ge$ 3.4$\times$.

\section{Gate 10: Windows-via-Wine}

\textbf{Measurement.} Sample 5 boards per lot, standard compatibility suite on 15 real Windows applications.

\textbf{Acceptance.} Compatibility and performance per application (Firefox 90\%/0.80$\times$; Chrome 90\%/0.80$\times$; MS Office 2016 85\%/0.80$\times$; MS Office 2019+ 60\%/0.75$\times$; LibreOffice 90\%/0.85$\times$; Photoshop CS6 80\%/0.75$\times$; Photoshop 2023 65\%/0.70$\times$; Steam games 65\%/0.75$\times$; AutoCAD 60\%/0.70$\times$; anti-cheat 0\%; native Linux 100\%/1.00$\times$; native RISC-V 100\%/1.15$\times$).

\textbf{Disposition.} Gate 10 is a benchmark gate, not a hardware gate. If Box64 efficiency is below 80\% on average, the ST GIPS claims drop proportionally. If compatibility is below 60\% on any listed application, that application is documented as "not recommended" in the product spec.

\section{Reliability Test Flow}

Sample testing per lot (every ~500 wafers):

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{HTOL:} 125~°C, 1000~h, 77 samples. Pass: $<$ 1 FIT after burn-in.
\item \textbf{HTSL:} 150~°C, 1000~h, 45 samples. Pass: no parametric drift $>$ 5\%.
\item \textbf{Thermal cycling:} $-$40 to $+$125~°C, 1000 cycles, 45 samples. Pass: no mechanical failure, no parametric drift $>$ 5\%.
\item \textbf{ESD:} HBM 2~kV, CDM 500~V, 3 samples. Pass: no hard failures.
\item \textbf{Retention:} 1000~h bake at 125~°C, 30 samples. Pass: extrapolated 10-year retention at 70~°C.
\item \textbf{Reflow simulation:} 260~°C, 150~s, 3 cycles, 30 samples. Pass: $<$ 2\% $P_r$ loss.
\item \textbf{FeFET-specific endurance:} $10^7$ cycle endurance at 85~°C, 15 samples. Pass: memory window $\ge$ 2.5~V after cycling.
\end{itemize}

FIT rate target: $<$ 10 at 85~°C after burn-in. Field return rate target: $<$ 1\%/year.

\section{Gate Pass-Rate Budget}

At mature D$_0$ = 0.1/cm$^2$, the cumulative yield through the gates:

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{2cm}p{2.5cm}p{2.5cm}p{3.5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Gate} & \textbf{Expected pass} & \textbf{Cumulative} & \textbf{Note} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Gate} & \textbf{Expected pass} & \textbf{Cumulative} & \textbf{Note} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Gate 1 & 90\% & 90\% & FeFET phase \\
Gate 2 & 95\% & 85.5\% & Single cell \\
Gate 3 & 90\% & 77\% & Small array \\
Gate 4 & 95\% & 73\% & Differential \\
Gate 5 & 90\% & 65.7\% & Full die \\
Gate 5b & 95\% & 62.4\% & Full memory \\
Gate 5c & 95\% & 59.3\% & DAC/TIA/ADC \\
Gate 6 & 98\% & 58.1\% & Package/board \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

The cumulative 58.1\% is the mature yield through Gates 1--6. The full-die yield at Gate 5 is the dominant loss (10\%); the remaining losses are small. First-year yield ($D_0$ = 0.3) through Gates 1--6 is ~35\%.

%=====================================================================
\part{Operating Procedures}
%=====================================================================

This part specifies the runtime behavior of the three products: boot sequence, address map, power states, DVFS, thermal management, error handling, OS support, Windows-via-Wine operation, and calibration.

\section{Address Map}

The RV64GCVH 64-bit address space is partitioned identically across all three products:

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{4cm}p{4.5cm}p{4cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Range} & \textbf{Contents} & \textbf{Attributes} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Range} & \textbf{Contents} & \textbf{Attributes} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x0000\_0000\_0000\_0000 & Boot ROM & Exec at reset; not cached \\
0x0000\_0000\_0000\_1000 & Boot SRAM (8 KB) & --- \\
0x0000\_0000\_4000\_0000 & MMIO & Uncached, L1/L2 bypassed \\
0x0000\_0000\_8000\_0000 & FeFET NVL3 window & 24--64 MB (per product) \\
0x0000\_0001\_0000\_0000 & DDR5 memory & Up to 256 GB (per product) \\
0x0000\_0002\_0000\_0000 & External flash (SPI) & Boot + config \\
0x0000\_0003\_0000\_0000 & PCIe device windows & Per PCIe enumeration \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

The MMIO region contains GPIO, CLINT (timers), PLIC-lite (interrupt controller), watchdog, thermal sensors, host interface (SPI for phone, PCIe config for PC/DC), DMA controller, vector block control (Ph-1 and Pc-1), OTP read, power status, security engine, debug authentication, and CXL control (Pc-1 and Dc-1S).

\section{Boot Sequence}

\subsection{Cold boot from NVL3}

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Power on.} Rails sequence per Part 7 (500~$\mu$s single-die to POR release).
\item \textbf{POR release.} Boot ROM executes from Boot SRAM at 500~MHz (temporary clock).
\item \textbf{Boot ROM (~1 ms).} PLL lock check, voltage reference check, NVL3 read check, DDR5 training (~200~ms), PCIe enumeration (PC/DC), CXL enumeration (if multi-socket), load ACPI tables, jump to OS loader.
\item \textbf{OS loader (Linux, ~50~ms).} Load kernel image, load initrd, pass ACPI tables to kernel.
\item \textbf{Linux kernel boot (~100~ms).} Initialize memory, enumerate CPUs, enumerate NUMA, initialize drivers, mount root filesystem.
\item \textbf{Userspace (~50~ms).} init/systemd starts, login prompt or desktop.
\end{enumerate}

Total cold boot to userspace: ~400~ms. Compare Intel i9-13900K with Windows 11: 25--40~s.

\subsection{Warm wake from sleep (P3 $\to$ P0)}

PLL relock (2~$\mu$s), boot ROM bypass (state preserved in NVL3), load critical OS pages from NVL3 (~5~$\mu$s), resume userspace (~3~$\mu$s). Total wake: ~10~$\mu$s. Compare Intel: 500~ms -- 2~s.

\section{Power States}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.5cm}p{4.5cm}p{3cm}p{3cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{State} & \textbf{Description} & \textbf{Wake time} & \textbf{Power (Pc-1)} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{State} & \textbf{Description} & \textbf{Wake time} & \textbf{Power (Pc-1)} \\
\midrule
\endhead
P0 & Full operation & --- & 182~W TDP \\
P1 & Reduced cores (50\%) & 10~ns & 100~W \\
P2 & Clock gated, PLL running & 10~ns & 30~W \\
P3 & Deep sleep (rails up, PLL off, SRAM retained, NVL3 retained) & 10~$\mu$s & 1.0~W \\
P4 & Power off (rails down) & 400~ms (cold boot) & 0.1~W \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

\section{Dynamic Voltage and Frequency Scaling (DVFS)}

P-states: P0 (6.2~GHz at 1.05~V, 182~W), P1 (5.4~GHz at 1.00~V, 130~W), P2 (4.2~GHz at 0.90~V, 70~W), P3 (2.8~GHz at 0.80~V, 35~W), P4 (1.8~GHz at 0.70~V, 18~W) for Pc-1. DVFS granularity: 100~MHz steps, 25~mV voltage steps. Transition time: 5~$\mu$s (PLL relock + voltage ramp). Controlled by OS via ACPI P-state interface.

Single-core boost is available when only one core is active: Ph-1 (4.5 → 4.8 GHz), Pc-1 (6.2 → 6.5 GHz), Dc-1S (6.0 → 6.3 GHz).

\section{Thermal Management}

Throttle thresholds: 85, 95, 105, 115~°C. Throttle policy: 75\%, 50\%, 25\%, emergency shutdown. Sensors: 4 (Ph-1), 6 (Pc-1), 8 (Dc-1S) bandgap, $\pm$1~°C accuracy, updated every 10~ms. Hysteresis: 5~°C. Emergency shutdown at 115~°C.

\section{Error Handling}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{4cm}p{3cm}p{5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Error type} & \textbf{Detection} & \textbf{Action} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Error type} & \textbf{Detection} & \textbf{Action} \\
\midrule
\endhead
NVL3 correctable ECC & SECDED single-bit & Correct, log, continue \\
NVL3 uncorrectable ECC & SECDED double-bit & Retry; mark bad block; use redundant column; report \\
L1/L2 ECC & SECDED & Correct, log, continue \\
L2 uncorrectable & 2-bit fail & Machine-check exception; ISR logs, retries \\
Register file SEU & Triple-redundant majority vote & Vote, continue \\
MMU page fault & Sv39 walk fails & Page fault exception; OS handler \\
Illegal instruction & Decoder & Illegal instruction exception \\
Misaligned load/store & Alignment check & Exception; software-emulated \\
CXL link error & CRC-8 per beat & Retry up to 2; fallback to reduced speed \\
PCIe link error & Per Gen5 spec & Log, retrain, retry TLP \\
PLL lock loss & Lock detector & PWR\_FAULT; watchdog POR \\
BOD trip & Multi-rail BOD & POR per sequencing \\
Thermal emergency & $>$115~°C & Emergency shutdown \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

\section{OS Support}

\textbf{Linux:} standard Linux (mainline kernel). Required kernel patches: RISC-V device tree (or ACPI) for the SoC, FeFET NVL3 driver, analog crossbar driver (Ph-1, Pc-1), multi-socket CXL enumeration, DDR5 memory controller driver. All mainline or in development. The FeFET NVL3 driver is released open source at production time.

\textbf{Android:} Android 15+ (or later). Required: RISC-V Android port, FeFET NVL3 driver, analog crossbar driver, camera ISP driver, 5G modem interface driver. The RISC-V Android port is in active development by Google and the RISC-V community.

\textbf{Windows:} Windows 11 itself does not install on RISC-V. Windows applications run via Wine + Box64. Forward-compatible if Microsoft ports Windows to RISC-V.

\section{Windows-via-Wine Step-by-Step}

The stack is Wine (Windows API on Linux), Box64/Box86 (x86 to RISC-V binary translation), and the Linux kernel. Installation: Wine via the Linux distribution's package manager or via Steam's Proton for games; Box64 alongside Wine. Running a Windows application: user double-clicks the .exe; Linux invokes Wine; Wine initializes Windows environment (~500~ms first time, ~50~ms cached); Wine invokes Box64; Box64 translates the entry point (~50~ms); Wine translates API calls; application window appears via X11 or Wayland + Wine's DirectX-to-Vulkan translation. Total launch time: 0.5--2~s simple apps, 2--5~s complex apps.

\section{Performance Counters}

Standard RISC-V performance counters (Zihpm), exposed to OS via \texttt{perf}: instructions retired, cycles, cache misses (L1, L2, SRAM L3, NVL3), branch mispredictions, TLB misses, DDR5 bandwidth, CXL traffic, thermal state, crossbar utilization.

\section{Calibration at Boot}

The hybrid loads calibration from OTP at every boot: Boot ROM (512~B), temperature table (344~B), bad-block map (2~KB), chip ID (16~B), NVL3 per-bank reference (320~B + scaled), vector LUT (35~KB for 512$^2$, 136~KB for 1024$^2$; Ph-1 and Pc-1 only), ADC dual-bank offset (32~B), per-column leak calibration (63~KB for Dc-1S, 167~KB for Ph-1 and Pc-1), vector temperature coefficients. Calibration load time at boot: ~5~ms (parallel from OTP to SRAM), hidden in the ~200~ms DDR5 training window.

\section{Factory Programming}

Pre-wake-up + first program + readback + re-program (up to 3 passes) + vector LUT calibration (Ph-1 and Pc-1 only) + leak calibration + OTP lock. Test time: 200~ms (Ph-1) to 500~ms (Dc-1S) per die.

%=====================================================================
\part{Performance}
%=====================================================================

This part presents the performance of the three Tier-1 products against Intel i9-13900K, Xeon Platinum 8480+, Xeon Gold 6338, Snapdragon 8 Gen 3, Snapdragon 8 Elite, and Apple A18 Pro.

\section{Benchmark Methodology}

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Single-thread:} SPECint~2017 (compiler + interpreter workloads), Cinebench R23 single-core
\item \textbf{Multi-thread:} Cinebench R23 multi-core, SPECint~2017\_rate, representative ML inference benchmarks
\item \textbf{AI throughput:} analog crossbar measured at the DAC, effective MAC/s after calibration
\item \textbf{Windows compatibility:} Box64 + Wine measured on 15 real Windows applications
\item \textbf{Power:} measured at the VRM input under each workload
\end{itemize}

The comparison baselines: Intel i9-13900K (24 threads, 5.8~GHz boost, 125~W TDP, 23.2~ST GIPS, 557~MT GIPS); Xeon Platinum 8480+ (56 cores, 3.8~GHz, 350~W TDP, ~23~ST GIPS, ~1400~MT GIPS); Xeon Gold 6338 (32 cores, 2.0~GHz, 205~W TDP, ~23~ST GIPS, ~1100~MT GIPS); Snapdragon 8 Gen 3 (8 cores, 3.3~GHz, ~18.4~ST GIPS); Snapdragon 8 Elite (8 cores, 4.32~GHz, ~25~ST GIPS); Apple A18 Pro (6 cores, ~4.05~GHz, ~24~ST GIPS).

\section{Single-Thread Performance}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{2.5cm}p{2cm}p{2cm}p{2.5cm}p{3cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Product} & \textbf{Clock} & \textbf{IPC} & \textbf{ST GIPS} & \textbf{vs Intel i9} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Product} & \textbf{Clock} & \textbf{IPC} & \textbf{ST GIPS} & \textbf{vs Intel i9} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Intel i9-13900K & 5.8~GHz & n/a & 23.2 & 1.00$\times$ \\
\midrule
Ph-1 & 4.5~GHz & 4.8 & 18.4 & 0.79$\times$ \\
Pc-1 & 6.2~GHz & 4.8 & 25.3 & 1.09$\times$ \\
Dc-1S & 6.0~GHz & 4.5 & 22.9 & 0.99$\times$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

ST GIPS uses the 85\% Box64 efficiency for translated applications; native RISC-V execution is at 100\% (multiply by 1/0.85 = 1.18). Native RISC-V ST GIPS: Ph-1 21.6, Pc-1 29.8, Dc-1S 26.9.

Ph-1 matches SD 8 Gen 3 on ST, is 26\% slower than SD 8 Elite, 23\% slower than A18 Pro. It beats SD 8 Gen 3 on MT (1.85$\times$), matches SD 8 Elite (1.06$\times$), and is 7\% below A18 Pro.

The 1024$^2$ crossbar achieves 4--6$\times$ Intel i9 for CNN and small transformer inference. The crossbar does not support LLM inference (2-bit Llama 7B/70B loses 11\% perplexity).

\section{Power Efficiency}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{2.5cm}p{2cm}p{2cm}p{2.5cm}p{2.5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Product} & \textbf{ST GIPS} & \textbf{TDP} & \textbf{GIPS/W} & \textbf{vs Intel i9} \\

\endfirsthead

\textbf{Product} & \textbf{ST GIPS} & \textbf{TDP} & \textbf{GIPS/W} & \textbf{vs Intel i9} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Intel i9-13900K & 23.2 & 125~W & 0.186 & 1.00$\times$ \\
\midrule
Ph-1 & 18.4 & 17~W & 1.08 & 5.8$\times$ \\
Pc-1 & 25.3 & 182~W & 0.139 & 0.75$\times$ \\
Dc-1S & 22.9 & 175~W & 0.131 & 0.70$\times$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

Phone wins efficiency (5.8$\times$ Intel). PC and DC lose efficiency (0.70--0.75$\times$ Intel) because they run higher clocks per core.

\section{Idle and Sleep Power}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{2cm}p{2cm}p{2cm}p{2.5cm}p{2cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{State} & \textbf{Pc-1} & \textbf{Intel i9} & \textbf{Ratio} & \textbf{Notes} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{State} & \textbf{Pc-1} & \textbf{Intel i9} & \textbf{Ratio} & \textbf{Notes} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Active P0 & 182~W & 125~W & 1.46$\times$ & --- \\
Idle P2 (clock gated) & 30~W & 15~W & 2.00$\times$ & --- \\
Sleep P3 (SRAM retained) & 1.0~W & 5~W & 0.20$\times$ & --- \\
Deep sleep P4 (NVL3 retained) & 0.1~W & 3~W & 0.033$\times$ & NVL3 non-volatile \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

At deep sleep, Pc-1 uses 30$\times$ less power than Intel i9-13900K because the NVL3 retains OS state without power.

\section{Wake Time}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{3cm}p{2.5cm}p{2.5cm}p{2cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Transition} & \textbf{Pc-1} & \textbf{Intel i9} & \textbf{Ratio} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Transition} & \textbf{Pc-1} & \textbf{Intel i9} & \textbf{Ratio} \\
\midrule
\endhead
P3 (sleep) $\to$ P0 (usable) & 10~$\mu$s & 500~ms -- 2~s & 50,000--200,000$\times$ \\
P4 (deep sleep) $\to$ P0 & 400~ms & 25--40~s & 60--100$\times$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

The P3 wake is the design's strongest user-facing feature: instant-on from sleep.

\section{Weaknesses}

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Phone ST vs top-tier:} Ph-1 at 18.4~GIPS matches SD 8 Gen 3 but loses to SD 8 Elite (25) and A18 Pro (24) by 23--26\%.
\item \textbf{DC absolute MT vs incumbents:} Dc-1S at 32 cores loses to Xeon Gold 6338 (32 cores) by 0.56$\times$ and to Xeon Platinum 8480+ (56 cores) by 0.44$\times$.
\item \textbf{Windows compatibility:} 80--90\% of Windows software runs; anti-cheat games and kernel-driver software do not.
\item \textbf{LLM inference:} crossbar does not support LLM inference (11\% perplexity loss on Llama 7B/70B).
\item \textbf{NVL3 write endurance:} 215 writes/cell lifetime, not suitable for write-heavy workloads.
\item \textbf{Power efficiency:} PC and DC products are 0.70--0.75$\times$ Intel on GIPS/W, requiring premium cooling.
\item \textbf{Pc-1 VRM:} the 234~W peak slightly exceeds standard 0.5~A/pin boards; premium boards recommended.
\end{itemize}

%=====================================================================
\part{Cost Analysis}
%=====================================================================

\section{Wafer and Die Cost}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{2cm}p{2cm}p{2cm}p{2cm}p{2cm}p{2.5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Die} & \textbf{Area} & \textbf{Dies/wafer} & \textbf{Yield (D$_0$=0.1)} & \textbf{Good dies} & \textbf{Cost/die} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Die} & \textbf{Area} & \textbf{Dies/wafer} & \textbf{Yield (D$_0$=0.1)} & \textbf{Good dies} & \textbf{Cost/die} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Phone & 150~mm$^2$ & 407 & 86.2\% & 350 & \$47.01 \\
PC & 292~mm$^2$ & 209 & 75.1\% & 157 & \$105.10 \\
DC & 205.9~mm$^2$ & 296 & 81.7\% & 242 & \$68.18 \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

At first-year yields ($D_0$ = 0.3): Phone 70.2\% (\$57.89/die), PC 41.2\% (\$175.53/die), DC 51.6\% (\$104.43/die). First-year pricing reflects this.

\item Dc-1S (\$707) is 94\% cheaper than Xeon Platinum 8480+ (\$11,804) over 3 years.

\end{itemize}

First-year pricing (at $D_0$ = 0.3/cm$^2$, ~28\% target margin) is 25--30\% higher than mature pricing: Ph-1 \$91, Pc-1 \$252, Dc-1S \$152. The first-year premium reflects the higher die cost at immature yield. Mature pricing (the prices in Part 8) applies once yield reaches $D_0$ = 0.1/cm$^2$.

%=====================================================================
\part{Security Features}
%=====================================================================

\section{Secure Boot Chain}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{3cm}p{3cm}p{3cm}p{3cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Stage} & \textbf{Signing key} & \textbf{Storage} & \textbf{Verification} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Stage} & \textbf{Signing key} & \textbf{Storage} & \textbf{Verification} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Boot ROM & Root key hash & Mask ROM (immutable) & Hardwired anchor \\
Bootloader & ECDSA P-256 & SPI flash & Verified by Boot ROM \\
Kernel & ECDSA P-256 & NVL3 / SPI flash & Verified by bootloader \\
Initrd & ECDSA P-256 & NVL3 & Verified by bootloader \\
Userspace & ECDSA P-256 & NVL3 / filesystem & Verified by kernel \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

Rollback protection: monotonic counter in OTP (4~B), incremented on each firmware update. A signed firmware with a counter value below the OTP value is rejected. Recovery mode: hardware allows re-flash from a signed recovery image, requires physical presence (mechanical button) + signed image.

\section{Memory Encryption}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{3cm}p{4cm}p{5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Region} & \textbf{Algorithm} & \textbf{Key source} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Region} & \textbf{Algorithm} & \textbf{Key source} \\
\midrule
\endhead
DDR5 & AES-XTS-256 & Per-boot key derived from device root key + DRAM package ID \\
LPDDR5X & AES-XTS-256 & Same \\
NVL3 & AES-XTS-256 & Separate per-boot key \\
L2 / L3 SRAM & Not encrypted (in-die) & --- \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

Key derivation: HKDF-SHA-256 from 256-bit device root key (in OTP) + boot nonce (in OTP, incremented on each cold boot) + DRAM package serial. Per-boot keys zeroized on power-down. Encryption engine cost: 1.5~mm$^2$, ~100~Gbps, ~50~mW active.

\section{Side-Channel Mitigations}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{4cm}p{5cm}p{3cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Threat} & \textbf{Mitigation} & \textbf{Performance cost} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Threat} & \textbf{Mitigation} & \textbf{Performance cost} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Meltdown & KPTI (kernel page-table isolation) & 5--15\% on syscall-heavy workloads \\
Spectre v1 & Array bounds speculation barriers & 2--5\% \\
Spectre v2 & Branch predictor hardening (IBRS or retpoline) & 5--10\% \\
Spectre v4 & Store-to-load forwarding barriers & 1--3\% \\
Rowhammer & DRAM refresh doubling & 1--2\% memory bandwidth \\
Cache-timing & Randomized cache indexing per process & 1--2\% \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

The microarchitectural mitigation set follows the RISC-V Security Model WG baseline. It costs ~10--15\% performance in the worst case, ~5\% typical.

\section{Attestation}

DICE (Device Identifier Composition Engine) per the TCG standard: boot ROM measures bootloader, bootloader measures kernel, kernel measures userspace. The measurement chain is hashed into a CDI per stage. The CDI is used to derive per-stage attestation keys. Signed quotes via ECDSA P-256 with device-unique key in OTP.

\section{Debug Authentication}

JTAG disabled by default. Unlock requires a signed challenge-response with a per-device key derived from the device root key. Unlock is time-limited (default 1 hour), re-locks automatically. All unlock events logged (in OTP event log or external BMC).

\section{Tamper Response}

Sensors: voltage (brown-out detector), temperature (bandgap thermal sensor), clock (glitch detector). On trip: zeroize memory encryption keys, lock debug, log event to OTP or BMC, optionally force reset.

\section{Hardware Crypto Engine}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{4cm}p{2cm}p{2.5cm}p{4cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Block} & \textbf{Area} & \textbf{Power} & \textbf{Function} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Block} & \textbf{Area} & \textbf{Power} & \textbf{Function} \\
\midrule
\endhead
AES-XTS-256 & 1.5~mm$^2$ & 50~mW active & Memory encryption \\
SHA-256/384 & 0.2~mm$^2$ & 5~mW active & Boot measurement, HKDF \\
ECDSA P-256 & 0.3~mm$^2$ & 10~mW active & Signature verify, attestation \\
TRNG & 0.05~mm$^2$ & 1~mW & Nonce generation \\
Clock glitch detector & 0.05~mm$^2$ & $<$1~mW & Tamper \\
\midrule
\textbf{Total security} & \textbf{2.1~mm$^2$} & \textbf{~66~mW active} & --- \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

\section{OTP Allocation}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{4.5cm}p{2cm}p{5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Item} & \textbf{Size} & \textbf{Notes} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Item} & \textbf{Size} & \textbf{Notes} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Root key hash (ECDSA public key) & 32~B & Immutable anchor \\
Device unique key (ECDSA private key) & 32~B & Per device \\
Debug public key & 32~B & OEM field-programmable \\
Rollback monotonic counter & 4~B & Incremented on update \\
Boot nonce & 16~B & Incremented on cold boot \\
Manufacturing data & 64~B & Lot, wafer, date, tester \\
Vector LUT calibration & 35--136~KB & Crossbar calibration (Ph-1, Pc-1 only) \\
NVL3 per-column calibration & 63--167~KB & NVL3 calibration \\
Reserved & 1.82~KB & --- \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

The security-only OTP allocation is 2~KB. The calibration OTP (vector LUT, NVL3 calibration) is larger and is used for the manufacturing calibration.

\section{Firmware Update}

Dual-bank A/B NVL3 firmware partitions (primary + backup, 64~KB each). Signed firmware image (ECDSA P-256). Update procedure: write new image to backup bank, verify signature, set boot pointer in OTP, reset. If new image fails to boot (watchdog), revert boot pointer to primary bank. Physical presence required for unsigned recovery.

\section{Hybrid-Specific Failure Modes}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{3.5cm}p{3cm}p{1.2cm}p{1.2cm}p{3cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Failure Mode} & \textbf{Cause} & \textbf{Severity} & \textbf{Likelihood} & \textbf{Mitigation} \\

\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Failure Mode} & \textbf{Cause} & \textbf{Severity} & \textbf{Likelihood} & \textbf{Mitigation} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Box64 poor efficiency & Suboptimal JIT & High & Medium & Benchmark-driven tuning \\
Windows app crash & Wine incompatibility & High & Medium & Compatibility list \\
MMU page fault storm & TLB thrash & Medium & Low & Larger TLB \\
Hypervisor boot fail & H ext bug & High & Low & KVM self-test \\
CXL enumeration fail & Protocol bug & High & Medium & Board respin \\
DDR5 training fail & Signal integrity & High & Low & Standard DDR5 IP \\
FeFET MEOL integration & N5 thermal budget & High & Medium & Foundry co-development \\
Multi-core scaling poor & L3 contention & Medium & Medium & Larger L3, better scheduler \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

\section{Highest-Risk Items}

The highest-likelihood, highest-severity hybrid-specific failures:

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{FeFET MEOL integration at N5} (Medium likelihood, High severity). This is the single largest open item. Mitigation: standard MEOL insertion pattern, 12--18 month process module development. Fallback: 22FDX FeFET chiplet or SRAM-only NVL3 (loses instant-boot).
\item \textbf{Box64 real-world efficiency} (Medium likelihood, High impact on ST GIPS claims). Mitigation: benchmark-driven tuning; if efficiency is 75\% instead of 85\%, ST GIPS drop 12\%.
\item \textbf{6.2~GHz N5 12-wide closure for Pc-1} (Medium likelihood, High impact on Pc-1 ST). Mitigation: fallback to 6.0~GHz (ST GIPS drops from 25.3 to 24.5).
\item \textbf{CXL 3.0 IP availability} (Low likelihood, High impact on multi-socket). Mitigation: IP vendors have confirmed availability; needs contract.
\end{enumerate}

%=====================================================================
\part{Health and Safety Notes}
%=====================================================================

This part notes any real and non-obvious health or safety hazards in the production or operation of the device. It does not state non-issues or common-knowledge items.

\section{Production Hazards}

\subsection{ALD precursor handling}

The FeFET fabrication uses atomic layer deposition (ALD) of hafnium and zirconium oxides. The ALD precursors (hafnium tetrakis-dimethylamide, Hf[N(CH$_3$)$_2$]$_4$, and zirconium tetrakis-dimethylamide, Zr[N(CH$_3$)$_2$]$_4$) are pyrophoric (air-reactive) and moisture-sensitive. They must be handled in a standard ALD precursor delivery system under inert atmosphere at the foundry. This is standard semiconductor fabrication practice; the precursors are not novel and are used in commercial HfO$_2$ production today. No new hazard is introduced by the FeFET process module beyond the existing hazards of standard CMOS ALD.

\subsection{Standard semiconductor hazards}

The fabrication flow uses the same process chemicals as standard N5 CMOS (HF, H$_2$SO$_4$, H$_2$O$_2$, NH$_3$, TiCl$_4$, WF$_6$, ozone, etc.). These are standard hazards of any advanced semiconductor fab, with standard facilities and protocols for handling, storage, and disposal. The FeFET module adds no new chemical hazard class.

\section{Operation Hazards}

\subsection{Electrical}

The finished device operates at 1.0~V (core), 1.8~V (I/O), 3.3~V (FeFET write), and up to ~1.05~V + current for the highest-power product. The highest-power product (Pc-1) draws up to ~234~W peak and ~17~A at 1.0~V. Standard precautions for high-current low-voltage DC power apply (proper PDN, fusing, thermal shutdown). No non-obvious electrical hazard is introduced.

\subsection{Thermal}

The highest-power product (Pc-1) requires liquid cooling at 0.15~°C/W. Standard precautions for liquid-cooled electronics apply (leak detection, water quality, non-conductive fluids, thermal shutdown at 115~°C). No non-obvious thermal hazard is introduced.

\subsection{Materials}

The finished device contains Hf$_{0.5}$Zr$_{0.5}$O$_2$ in the FeFET cells. This is a solid oxide film, non-toxic, non-reactive, and non-hazardous under normal operation. It is not released into the environment under normal use. Under e-waste disposal, it is disposed of as standard semiconductor waste.

No non-obvious health hazard to the end user is introduced by the FeFET cell or the finished device.

\section{Summary}

The design introduces no novel user-facing health or safety hazard. The production-side ALD precursor handling is standard semiconductor practice. The operation-side electrical, thermal, and material exposures are standard for high-performance semiconductor products. No additional precautions beyond standard semiconductor and high-power electronics practice are required.

\part{Advanced Optimization and Higher-Tier Products}
%=====================================================================

This part presents the two product-level upgrade paths available beyond the three Tier-1 products specified in Parts 2--16: the advanced optimization configurations (AO1 and AO2) and the higher-tier product variants (Ph-2, Pc-2, Dc-1, Dc-2). Both paths preserve the shared architecture (FeFET cell, NVL3 subsystem, crossbar, CPU core family, fabrication process, security features) and offer additional performance, capacity, or cores at a price premium.

The complete engineering specifications for every upgrade path are available from the author.

\section{The Two Upgrade Paths}

Two product-level upgrade paths are available:

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{2.5cm}p{4.5cm}p{3cm}p{2.5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Upgrade path} & \textbf{What it adds} & \textbf{Products} & \textbf{Price premium} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Upgrade path} & \textbf{What it adds} & \textbf{Products} & \textbf{Price premium} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Advanced Optimization (AO1) & 2$\times$ NVL3 capacity; 4-bit signed crossbar & Ph-1, Pc-1, Dc-1S & +5\% \\
Advanced Optimization (AO2) & 4$\times$ NVL3 capacity; 8-bit signed crossbar & Ph-1, Pc-1, Dc-1S & +15\% \\
Higher-Tier Products & Higher clocks, higher core count & Ph-2, Pc-2, Dc-1, Dc-2 & +17\% to +268\% \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

The two upgrade paths can be combined. A higher-tier product can be specified with either AO1 or AO2. The combined product's price is the sum of the two premiums; the engineering specifications for the combined products are available from the author.

\section{Advanced Optimization Configurations (AO1 and AO2)}

\begin{tcolorbox}[title=\textbf{ADVANCED OPTIMIZATION AVAILABLE}, colback=green!5, colframe=green!70!70, width=\textwidth]

The design specified in Parts 2--16 is the baseline (2-bit signed crossbar; single-level NVL3).

\textbf{Two advanced optimization configurations are available.}

\textbf{AO1 (advanced optimization 1)} provides:
\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{NVL3 capacity:} 200\% of baseline
    \item \textbf{Crossbar weight precision:} 4-bit signed
    \item \textbf{AI accuracy:} matches FP32 on ResNet-50 and VGG-16; within 1 percentage point on MobileNet-v2 and BERT-base
    \item \textbf{NVL3 \$/MB:} 53\% of baseline
    \item \textbf{Price:} +5\% over baseline
    \item \textbf{CPU, power, TDP, package, cooling, memory, I/O, security, fabrication:} unchanged from baseline
\end{itemize}

\textbf{AO2 (advanced optimization 2)} provides:
\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{NVL3 capacity:} 400\% of baseline
    \item \textbf{Crossbar weight precision:} 8-bit signed
    \item \textbf{AI accuracy:} matches FP32 on ResNet-50, VGG-16, MobileNet-v2, and BERT-base
    \item \textbf{NVL3 \$/MB:} 29\% of baseline
    \item \textbf{Price:} +15\% over baseline
    \item \textbf{CPU, power, TDP, package, cooling, memory, I/O, security, fabrication:} unchanged from baseline
\end{itemize}

The complete specifications for the advanced optimization configurations are available from the author.

\end{tcolorbox}

\section{Higher-Tier Product Variants}

\begin{tcolorbox}[title=\textbf{HIGHER-TIER PRODUCTS AVAILABLE}, colback=blue!5, colframe=blue!70!70, width=\textwidth]

The design specified in Parts 2--16 includes three Tier-1 products (Ph-1, Pc-1, Dc-1S).

\textbf{Four higher-tier product variants are available beyond the Tier-1 products.}

\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{Ph-2 (gaming phone):} 6 big + 4 little cores at 5.2~GHz boost / 4.0~GHz sustained. 15\% more ST and 39\% more MT than Ph-1. Requires a gaming vapor chamber. \$82.
    \item \textbf{Pc-2 (maximum-performance desktop):} 36 cores at 6.5~GHz boost / 6.0~GHz sustained. 5\% more ST and 26\% more MT than Pc-1. Requires a 360~mm AIO liquid cooler and a premium LGA 1700 board with a 1~A/pin VRM. \$189.
    \item \textbf{Dc-1 (mid-tier server):} 64 cores at 6.0~GHz boost / 5.5~GHz sustained. 100\% more MT than Dc-1S. Requires a 0.10~°C/W cold plate with facility chilled water. \$380.
    \item \textbf{Dc-2 (AI inference server):} 96 cores at 6.0~GHz single-core boost / 5.4~GHz all-core boost / 5.0~GHz sustained. 161\% more MT than Dc-1S. Requires a 0.06~°C/W cold plate with facility chilled water. \$570.
\end{itemize}

The complete specifications for the higher-tier products are available from the author.

\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection{What is unchanged}

The higher-tier products share the Tier-1 baseline's fabrication process (N5 CMOS with FeFET MEOL), FeFET cell, NVL3 subsystem, crossbar (Ph-2 and Pc-2 only), CPU core family, security features, failure modes, and health and safety notes. The engineering principles that underlie the Tier-1 baseline apply to the higher-tier products with the clock, core-count, or additional-core differences stated above.

\subsection{Combinations with advanced optimization}

Each of the four higher-tier products can be combined with either the AO1 or AO2 advanced optimization configuration. The combined products carry both the higher-tier performance improvement and the advanced optimization capacity and precision improvements. The complete specifications for the combined products are available from the author.

\part*{Appendix A: Register Map}
\addcontentsline{toc}{part}{Appendix A: Register Map}
%=====================================================================

\section{Conventions}

Every custom MMIO block in this design is described in this appendix. The standard RISC-V CLINT (0x4000\_1000), the DDR5 controller, the PCIe root complex, and the CXL host bridge follow their respective industry specifications and are not repeated here.

\textbf{Register access codes:} RO = read-only, RW = read-write, WO = write-only (read returns 0), W1C = write 1 to clear.

\textbf{Reset values} are the value after cold power-on before any firmware write, unless stated otherwise (some blocks have OTP-derived reset values that are programmed at factory test).

\textbf{Register width} is 32 bits unless stated otherwise. 64-bit registers occupy two consecutive 32-bit slots, low word at the lower offset.

\textbf{Reserved bits} must be written as 0 and are read as 0.

\textbf{Base addresses:} every block occupies a 4~KB page. Blocks with multiple instances (per-channel, per-bank, per-sensor) place instance $n$ at base $+ n \times$ page size.

\section{MMIO Base Address Map}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{2.5cm}p{3cm}p{3cm}p{5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Base} & \textbf{Block} & \textbf{Instances} & \textbf{Products} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Base} & \textbf{Block} & \textbf{Instances} & \textbf{Products} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x4000\_0000 & GPIO & 1 & All \\
0x4000\_1000 & CLINT (standard) & 1 & All \\
0x4000\_2000 & PLIC-lite & 1 & All \\
0x4000\_3000 & Watchdog & 1 & All \\
0x4000\_4000 & Thermal & 4 (Ph-1), 6 (Pc-1), 8 (Dc-1S) & All \\
0x4000\_5000 & Host interface & 1 & All \\
0x4000\_6000 & DMA & 1 & All \\
0x4000\_7000 & Vector block & 1 & Ph-1, Pc-1 \\
0x4000\_8000 & OTP & 1 & All \\
0x4000\_9000 & Power status & 1 & All \\
0x4000\_A000 & Security engine & 1 & All \\
0x4000\_B000 & Debug authentication & 1 & All \\
0x4000\_C000 & CXL control & 1 & Pc-1, Dc-1S \\
0x4000\_D000 & NVL3 controller & 1 & All \\
0x4000\_E000 & Crossbar controller & 1 & Ph-1, Pc-1 \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

Writes to unimplemented addresses in a block's 4~KB page return 0 on read and are silently ignored on write (no bus error). This allows firmware to probe for block presence by writing a known pattern and reading back.

\section{GPIO (0x4000\_0000)}

8 pins on Ph-1; 32 pins on Pc-1 and Dc-1S. Only the first 8 DATA/DIR/IE/IS/TRIG/IC/DRIVE/PULL/SLEW/INSYNC bits are meaningful on Ph-1.

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.4cm}p{2cm}p{1cm}p{1.5cm}p{7cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x00 & DATA & RW & 0 & [n] pin n output value (only when DIR[n]=1) \\
0x04 & DIR & RW & 0 & [n]=1 output, [n]=0 input \\
0x08 & IE & RW & 0 & [n]=1 interrupt enabled \\
0x0C & IS & RO & 0 & [n]=1 interrupt pending \\
0x10 & TRIG & RW & 0 & [2n:2n+1]: 00=low, 01=high, 10=fall, 11=rise \\
0x14 & IC & W1C & --- & Write 1 to clear IS[n] \\
0x18 & DRIVE & RW & 0x55555555 & [2n:2n+1]: 00=2~mA, 01=4~mA, 10=8~mA, 11=12~mA \\
0x1C & PULL & RW & 0 & [2n:2n+1]: 00=none, 01=up, 10=down, 11=keeper \\
0x20 & SLEW & RW & 0 & [n]=1 fast slew, [n]=0 slow \\
0x24 & INSYNC & RW & 0 & [2n:2n+1]: 00=0, 01=2, 10=4, 11=8 cycle synchronizer delay \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

On Ph-1, bits [31:8] of every register are reserved and read 0.

\section{PLIC-lite (0x4000\_2000)}

Custom variant of the RISC-V PLIC. 16 interrupt sources on Ph-1; 64 on Pc-1 and Dc-1S. Sources are assigned in Appendix C. Register layout follows the standard RISC-V PLIC specification except where noted.

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.4cm}p{2.5cm}p{1cm}p{1.5cm}p{7cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x0000 & PRIORITY[0] & RW & 0 & Priority of source 0 (0=disabled) \\
... & PRIORITY[n] & RW & 0 & 4~B per source; n = 0..N\_SOURCE-1 \\
0x1000 & PENDING & RO & 0 & 32-bit word per 32 sources; bit n = source n \\
0x2000 & ENABLE & RW & 0 & 32-bit word per 32 sources; bit n = source n enabled for hart 0 \\
0x200000 & THRESHOLD & RW & 0 & Hart 0 threshold (only hart 0 in this implementation) \\
0x200004 & CLAIM & RW & --- & Read returns highest-priority pending source; write same value to complete \\
0x200008 & CFG & RW & 0 & [0]=enable PLIC; [1]=interrupt mode (0=level, 1=edge) \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

Only hart 0 is supported; firmware running on other harts must route interrupts through hart 0 software interrupt (source 1). This is the single-hart-PLIC design trade-off.

\section{Watchdog (0x4000\_3000)}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.4cm}p{2cm}p{1cm}p{1.5cm}p{7cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x00 & CTRL & RW & 0 & [0] ENABLE; [1] FEED (WO, self-clear); [2] PRESCALE\_EN; [4:3] TIMEOUT\_SEL (00=1~ms, 01=10~ms, 10=100~ms, 11=1~s); [5] IRQ\_EN; [6] RESET\_EN; [7] FORCE\_RESET (WO); [8] CLR\_RESET\_CAUSE (WO) \\
0x04 & STATUS & RO & 0x2 & [0] RUNNING; [1] READY; [2] TIMEOUT; [3] RESET\_OCCURRED; [4] RS:SW; [5] RS:WDT; [6] RS:BOD; [7] RS:THERMAL; [8] RS:EXTERNAL \\
0x08 & LOAD & RW & 0xFFFFFFFF & Timeout load (prescaler cycles) \\
0x0C & COUNT & RO & 0xFFFFFFFF & Current count \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

Reset cause bits [8:4] are latched from the last reset event and cleared only by writing CLR\_RESET\_CAUSE. They are the source of truth for boot-time diagnostic logging.

\section{Thermal Sensors (0x4000\_4000)}

Per-sensor registers: instance $n$ at base $+ n \times 0x100$. Number of sensors: 4 (Ph-1), 6 (Pc-1), 8 (Dc-1S).

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.4cm}p{2cm}p{1cm}p{1.5cm}p{7cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x00 & RAW & RO & --- & 16-bit signed raw count; convert with CAL\_OFF + CAL\_SLOPE \\
0x04 & TEMP\_mC & RO & --- & Temperature in milli-°C, already calibrated (formula below) \\
0x08 & CAL\_OFF & RW & OTP & Calibration offset (OTP-programmed) \\
0x0C & CAL\_SLOPE & RW & OTP & Calibration slope (OTP-programmed) \\
0x10 & THRESH & RW & 0x55F0 & Threshold in °C×100 (default 85°C×100 = 0x21F4) \\
0x14 & HYST & RW & 500 & Hysteresis in °C×100 (default 5°C) \\
0x18 & CTRL & RW & 0x1 & [0] ENABLE; [1] IRQ\_EN; [2] TRIP\_EN \\
0x1C & STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] VALID; [1] ABOVE\_THRESH; [2] TRIP\_LATCHED (W1C) \\
0x20 & TRIP\_THRESH & RW & 0x5DC0 & Emergency shutdown threshold in °C×100 (default 115°C×100 = 0x5DC0) \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

Conversion: `TEMP_mC = (RAW × CAL_SLOPE) / 1024 + CAL_OFF`. The sensor is a bandgap PTAT type with ±1°C accuracy over 0--125°C after the two-point calibration.

\section{Host Interface (0x4000\_5000)}

SPI slave on Ph-1; PCIe endpoint config space on Pc-1 and Dc-1S. Registers below are common to both; per-product differences are noted.

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.4cm}p{2.5cm}p{1cm}p{1.5cm}p{7cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x00 & CTRL & RW & 0 & [0] IF\_ENABLE; [1] INT\_MODE (SPI: level/edge; PCIe: MSI/MSI-X); [2] DMA\_EN \\
0x04 & STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] LINK\_UP; [1] BUSY; [2] ERROR; [3] DMA\_ACTIVE \\
0x08 & IRQ\_MASK & RW & 0 & Per-bit mask (mirrors PLIC source enable) \\
0x0C & IRQ\_PENDING & RO & 0 & Raw pending bits from PLIC (read-only mirror) \\
0x10 & DMA\_CMD\_ADDR & RW & 0 & DMA descriptor list base address (host memory) \\
0x14 & DMA\_CMD\_LEN & RW & 0 & Number of 16~B descriptors \\
0x18 & DMA\_CMD\_TRIGGER & WO & --- & Write 1 to start DMA descriptor processing \\
0x1C & DMA\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] BUSY; [1] DONE; [2] ERROR; [3] ABORT \\
0x20 & FW\_VERSION & RO & OTP & Firmware version (OTP-programmed at factory) \\
0x24 & FW\_TRIGGER & WO & --- & Write magic 0x4E564C33 to reload firmware image from NVL3 \\
0x28 & PCIE\_DEVICE\_ID & RO & 0x106D & PCI device ID (Pc-1, Dc-1S only) \\
0x2C & PCIE\_VENDOR\_ID & RO & 0x1D1F & PCI vendor ID (Pc-1, Dc-1S only) \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

On Ph-1, offsets 0x28 and 0x2C read 0 (no PCIe). On Pc-1 and Dc-1S, offsets 0x00--0x24 are used for the internal DMA path; the standard PCIe config space at the PHY level (BARs, MSI-X table) is beyond this block's scope.

\section{DMA Controller (0x4000\_6000)}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.4cm}p{2.5cm}p{1cm}p{1.5cm}p{7cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x00 & CTRL & RW & 0 & [0] ENABLE; [1] LOOP\_EN; [2] IRQ\_EN \\
0x04 & STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] BUSY; [1] DONE; [2] ERROR; [3] ABORTED \\
0x08 & SRC\_ADDR & RW & 0 & Source address (low 32 bits) \\
0x0C & SRC\_ADDR\_HI & RW & 0 & Source address (high 32 bits) \\
0x10 & DST\_ADDR & RW & 0 & Destination address (low 32 bits) \\
0x14 & DST\_ADDR\_HI & RW & 0 & Destination address (high 32 bits) \\
0x18 & LEN & RW & 0 & Transfer length in bytes (multiple of 8) \\
0x1C & CHUNK & RW & 4096 & Chunk size in bytes (per burst) \\
0x20 & TRIGGER & WO & --- & Write 1 to start transfer \\
0x24 & ABORT & WO & --- & Write 1 to abort (DMA returns to idle within 1~µs) \\
0x28 & CUR\_ADDR & RO & 0 & Current source address (progress) \\
0x2C & CUR\_REMAIN & RO & 0 & Remaining bytes \\
0x30 & ERROR\_CODE & RO & 0 & [3:0] last error (1=src bus, 2=dst bus, 3=alignment, 4=timeout, 5=abort) \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

One DMA engine, programmed from hart 0. Multiple channels are software-arbitrated via the LOOP\_EN bit and a software descriptor queue in SRAM.

\section{Vector Block Control (0x4000\_7000)}

Ph-1 and Pc-1 only. This block controls the analog crossbar's functional DAC/ADC chain (Part 5 §5.13 custom instructions trigger the compute; this block configures the chain).

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.4cm}p{2.5cm}p{1cm}p{1.5cm}p{7cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x00 & VECMODE & RW & 0 & [1:0] mode: 00=idle, 01=2-bit, 10=4-bit (AO1), 11=8-bit (AO2) \\
0x04 & DAC\_CODE & RW & 0 & [3:0] DAC code for current row (used by crossbar.macc) \\
0x08 & FIFO\_WR & RW & --- & Write 8~B input vector word (pushed to input FIFO) \\
0x0C & FIFO\_WR\_COUNT & RO & 0 & Number of words currently in input FIFO \\
0x10 & FIFO\_RD & RO & --- & Read 8~B output vector word (popped from output FIFO) \\
0x14 & FIFO\_RD\_COUNT & RO & 0 & Number of words currently in output FIFO \\
0x18 & FIFO\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] IN\_FULL; [1] IN\_EMPTY; [2] OUT\_FULL; [3] OUT\_EMPTY; [4] OVERFLOW \\
0x1C & CTRL & RW & 0 & [0] ENABLE; [1] AUTO\_TRIGGER; [2] IRQ\_EN \\
0x20 & STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] BUSY; [1] MAC\_DONE; [2] ERROR \\
0x24 & TIA\_GAIN & RW & 0 & [1:0] gain: 00=high (2.6~kΩ), 01=mid, 10=low (10~kΩ), 11=reserved \\
0x28 & ADC\_CONFIG & RW & 0 & [1:0] resolution: 00=8-bit, 01=10-bit (default) \\
0x2C & CALIB\_CTRL & RW & 0 & [0] START\_CALIB; [1] ABORT\_CALIB; [2] LOAD\_FROM\_OTP \\
0x30 & CALIB\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] BUSY; [1] DONE; [2] ERROR; [3] OTP\_LOADED \\
0x34 & CALIB\_COL & RW & 0 & Column index for calibration (0--511 or 0--1023) \\
0x38 & CALIB\_ROW & RW & 0 & Row index for calibration (0--511 or 0--1023) \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

The four custom instructions (`crossbar.ld`, `.st`, `.macc`, `.wait`) have hardware backing in this block: `.macc` triggers a MAC cycle, `.wait` polls MAC\_DONE. Direct register access to FIFO\_WR and FIFO\_RD is used only during initialization and calibration.

\section{OTP Controller (0x4000\_8000)}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.4cm}p{2.5cm}p{1cm}p{1.5cm}p{7cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x00 & ADDR & RW & 0 & OTP word address (32-bit words) \\
0x04 & DATA\_READ & RO & 0 & Data at ADDR (valid after READ\_STROBE) \\
0x08 & DATA\_WRITE & RW & 0 & Data to write at ADDR \\
0x0C & READ\_STROBE & WO & --- & Write 1 to start read; STATUS.RDY set when data valid \\
0x10 & PROG\_STROBE & WO & --- & Write 1 to start program; STATUS.PROG\_DONE set when complete \\
0x14 & CTRL & RW & 0 & [0] PROG\_ENABLE (must be 1 for program; requires VPP if fuse-based) \\
0x18 & STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] RDY; [1] PROG\_DONE; [2] PROG\_FAILED; [3] LOCKED (OTP region is locked) \\
0x1C & LOCK & WO & --- & Write 1 to permanently lock OTP (irreversible) \\
0x20 & LOCK\_STATUS & RO & OTP & [n]=1 means region n locked; regions defined in §14.7 \\
0x24 & READ\_BACK & RO & 0 & Last-read value (for verification) \\
0x28 & REGION\_BASE & RW & 0 & [7:0] current region base (0=root key, 1=device, ..., 7=calibration) \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

Programming sequence: write ADDR, write DATA\_WRITE, write CTRL.PROG\_ENABLE=1, write PROG\_STROBE=1, poll STATUS.PROG\_DONE (or PROG\_FAILED), verify via READ\_STROBE and compare to DATA\_WRITE. Programming requires the OTP controller to have been unlocked at boot by the security engine (see Appendix B §B.7).

\section{Power Status and Sequencing (0x4000\_9000)}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.4cm}p{2.5cm}p{1cm}p{1.5cm}p{7cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x00 & RAIL\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [n]=1 rail n up. n=0 core, 1 I/O, 2 analog, 3 PLL, 4 LPDDR5X/DDR5, 5 auxiliary \\
0x04 & RAIL\_OK & RO & 0 & [n]=1 rail n within spec \\
0x08 & SEQUENCE\_STATE & RO & 0 & [3:0] state (0=off, 1=core, 2=io, 3=analog, 4=pll, 5=mem, 6=aux, 7=up, 8=shutdown) \\
0x0C & BOD\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [n]=1 brown-out detected on rail n (latched) \\
0x10 & BOD\_CLR & W1C & --- & Clear BOD\_STATUS \\
0x14 & SHUTDOWN\_TRIGGER & WO & --- & Write 0xDEAD to trigger orderly shutdown \\
0x18 & RESET\_CAUSE & RO & 0 & Same encoding as Watchdog STATUS[8:4] \\
0x1C & RESET\_CLR & WO & --- & Write 1 to clear RESET\_CAUSE \\
0x20 & DVFS\_CTRL & RW & 0 & [3:0] target P-state (0=P0, 1=P1, ..., 4=P4); [8] REQUEST; [9] AUTO (hardware DVFS) \\
0x24 & DVFS\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [3:0] current P-state; [8] TRANSITIONING; [9] AUTO\_ACTIVE \\
0x28 & DVFS\_TRIM\_V & RW & 0 & [7:0] voltage trim (signed, 25~mV/LSB) \\
0x2C & DVFS\_TRIM\_F & RW & 0 & [7:0] frequency trim (signed, 100~MHz/LSB) \\
0x30 & THROTTLE & RO & 0 & [1:0] throttle level (0=none, 1=75%, 2=50%, 3=25%); [2] THERMAL\_SHUTDOWN \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

DVFS sequence: write DVFS\_CTRL with target P-state and REQUEST=1; poll DVFS\_STATUS until TRANSITIONING clears. Transition takes 5 µs nominal, 50 µs worst case (PLL relock + voltage ramp). The AUTO bit lets hardware choose the P-state from thermal and utilization; software DISABLEs AUTO by writing 0.

... & ECDSA\_S & RO & 0 & Signature component s (32~B) \\
0xD8 & TRNG\_CTRL & RW & 0 & [0] ENABLE \\
0xDC & TRNG\_DATA & RO & 0 & 4 random bytes per read (FIFO depth 64) \\
0xE0 & TRNG\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] READY; [1] HEALTH\_FAIL \\
0xE4 & DICE\_CDI & RO & 0 & Compound Device Identifier (16~B at 0xE4--0xF3) \\
0xF4 & DICE\_MEASURE & RW & 0 & Next measurement source address (low) \\
0xF8 & DICE\_MEASURE\_HI & RW & 0 & High 32 bits \\
0xFC & DICE\_CTRL & RW & 0 & [0] START; [1] UPDATE \\
0x100 & DICE\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] BUSY; [1] DONE; [2] ERROR \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

AES-XTS key slots are write-only and read back 0. Key material is loaded only at boot, from the ROM to the security engine via the OTP controller, never via software-visible paths. The DICE CDI is regenerated on every cold boot from the OTP device key + boot nonce + first measurement.

\section{Debug Authentication (0x4000\_B000)}

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.4cm}p{2.5cm}p{1cm}p{1.5cm}p{7cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x00 & CHALLENGE & RW & 0 & 32~B challenge written by debugger (write only; 0x00--0x1F) \\
0x20 & RESPONSE & RO & 0 & 32~B response read by debugger (0x20--0x3F) \\
0x40 & CTRL & RW & 0 & [0] GEN\_RESPONSE; [1] VERIFY; [2] UNLOCK; [3] LOCK \\
0x44 & STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] BUSY; [1] DONE; [2] VERIFY\_OK; [3] UNLOCKED; [4] TIMEOUT; [5] LOCKED \\
0x48 & TIMEOUT & RW & 3600 & Unlock duration in seconds (default 3600 = 1 hour); 0 = unlimited \\
0x4C & ATTEMPTS & RO & 0 & Failed unlock attempts (counter) \\
0x50 & LOCK\_DOWN & RO & 0 & [0]=1 if locked permanently after threshold attempts \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

Unlock sequence: debugger generates random 32~B challenge, writes to CHALLENGE; hardware computes ECDSA signature of the challenge with the device-unique private key in OTP; debugger reads RESPONSE and verifies against the public key. If verification succeeds, debugger writes VERIFY=1 followed by UNLOCK=1; hardware opens JTAG for TIMEOUT seconds. Failed verifications increment ATTEMPTS; after 5 failed attempts, LOCK\_DOWN is set and unlock requires factory RMA.

\section{CXL Control (0x4000\_C000)}

Pc-1 and Dc-1S only. Base registers; the full standard CXL config space is separate.

\begin{center}

\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

The CXL block controls the link and NUMA topology only. The actual memory window (for CXL.mem targets) is configured via the standard CXL config space at 0x0000\_0003\_xxxx\_xxxx; that path is documented in the CXL 3.0 specification and not repeated here.

\section{NVL3 Controller (0x4000\_D000)}

The NVL3 controller manages the FeFET non-volatile L3 array. The data window at 0x0000\_0000\_8000\_0000 routes cache-line reads and writes through the controller's row/page logic; the control registers below configure the controller.

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.4cm}p{2.5cm}p{1cm}p{1.5cm}p{7cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x00 & CTRL & RW & 0 & [0] ENABLE; [1] START\_WRITE\_VERIFY; [2] START\_ERASE; [3] START\_PRE\_WAKEUP; [4] RESET; [5] WRITE\_BUF\_EN \\
0x04 & STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] READY; [1] BUSY; [2] ERROR; [3] VERIFY\_FAILED; [4] ENDURANCE\_WARN; [5] CALIB\_IN\_PROGRESS \\
0x08 & ROW\_ADDR & RW & 0 & Row address (0--4095) \\
0x0C & BANK\_ADDR & RW & 0 & Bank address (0--255) \\
0x10 & DATA\_LOW & RW & 0 & 256 bits of write data, low (offsets 0x10--0x2F) \\
0x30 & DATA\_HIGH & RW & 0 & 256 bits of write data, high (offsets 0x30--0x4F) \\
0x50 & RDATA\_LOW & RO & 0 & 256 bits of read data, low \\
0x70 & RDATA\_HIGH & RO & 0 & 256 bits of read data, high \\
0x90 & WRITE\_VERIFY\_CTRL & RW & 0x302 & [3:0] max retries (default 3); [11:8] pulse width (default 2 = 25~ns) \\
0x94 & WRITE\_VERIFY\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [7:0] attempt count; [8] SUCCESS; [9] FAILED \\
0x98 & ECC\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] SINGLE\_BIT; [1] DOUBLE\_BIT; [8] OVERFLOW \\
0x9C & ECC\_ADDR & RO & 0 & Address of the last ECC event (row << 16 | bank) \\
0xA0 & BAD\_BLOCK & RO & 0 & [15:0] bad-block count; [16] last-bad-block-address-available \\
0xA4 & BAD\_BLOCK\_ADDR & RO & 0 & Last bad-block address \\
0xA8 & WEAR\_COUNT\_LOW & RO & 0 & Cumulative write count (low 32) \\
0xAC & WEAR\_COUNT\_HIGH & RO & 0 & Cumulative write count (high 32) \\
0xB0 & PRE\_WAKEUP\_CTRL & RW & 0x3E8 & [15:0] pulse count per cell (default 1000 = 0x3E8) \\
0xB4 & PRE\_WAKEUP\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] DONE; [1] BUSY; [2] ERROR \\
0xB8 & CAL\_TABLE\_BASE & RW & OTP & Calibration table base address in SRAM \\
0xBC & CAL\_TABLE\_LEN & RW & OTP & Calibration table length in 4~B words \\
0xC0 & REF\_OFFSET\_BASE & RW & OTP & Per-column reference offset base (63--167 KB table) \\
0xC4 & CALIB\_START & WO & --- & Write 1 to (re)load calibration from OTP \\
0xC8 & CALIB\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] DONE; [1] BUSY; [2] ERROR \\
0xCC & ERROR\_LOG & RO & 0 & [7:0] last error code (1=verify, 2=ecc, 3=endurance, 4=bad-block-limit, 5=calibration) \\
0xD0 & IRQ\_ENABLE & RW & 0 & [0] ECC\_SB; [1] ECC\_DB; [2] VERIFY; [3] ENDURANCE; [4] BAD\_BLOCK \\
0xD4 & IRQ\_STATUS & RO & 0 & Mirrors IRQ\_ENABLE bit layout; latched until IC write \\
0xD8 & IC & W1C & --- & Clear bits in IRQ\_STATUS \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\end{center}

Write sequence (single row): write ROW\_ADDR, BANK\_ADDR, DATA\_LOW (8×32 bits), DATA\_HIGH (8×32 bits); write CTRL.START\_WRITE\_VERIFY; poll STATUS.BUSY or wait for VERIFY interrupt; check WRITE\_VERIFY\_STATUS.SUCCESS (or FAILED). The controller internally performs erase, program, read-verify, and retry up to max-retries.

Read sequence (single row): write ROW\_ADDR, BANK\_ADDR; write CTRL.START\_WRITE\_VERIFY is NOT used for reads; instead, reads are triggered by the data-window access at 0x8000\_0000. Alternatively, direct controller read: write ROW\_ADDR, BANK\_ADDR, then read RDATA\_LOW / RDATA\_HIGH after the controller's page-open latency. The controller's direct read path is provided for bring-up and ECC scrub.

Pre-wake-up: write PRE\_WAKEUP\_CTRL with the desired pulse count (default 1000); write CTRL.START\_PRE\_WAKEUP; poll PRE\_WAKEUP\_STATUS.DONE. Pre-wake-up must be applied to every bank before the NVL3 data region is used as persistent storage. Factory test applies 10^3 pulses to the NVL3 data region and 10^4 to the microcode region.

Calibration: at factory test and after any NVL3 configuration change, write CALIB\_START; poll CALIB\_STATUS.DONE. The controller reads the calibration table from OTP, populates REF\_OFFSET, and validates each column's reference against a known pattern.

\section{Crossbar Controller (0x4000\_E000)}

Ph-1 and Pc-1 only. Full functional configuration of the analog crossbar. The compute path is the custom-instruction interface (`crossbar.macc` etc.); this block configures the analog chain and manages weight updates and calibration.

\begin{center}
\begin{longtable}{p{1.4cm}p{2.5cm}p{1cm}p{1.5cm}p{7cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
\textbf{Offset} & \textbf{Name} & \textbf{Access} & \textbf{Reset} & \textbf{Bit fields} \\
\midrule
\endhead
0x00 & CTRL & RW & 0 & [0] ENABLE; [1] W+/W−-MODE (0=Option B time-mux, 1=Option A parallel); [2] AUTO\_DAC; [3] IRQ\_EN \\
0x04 & STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] READY; [1] BUSY; [2] ERROR; [3] MAC\_DONE; [4] CALIB\_BUSY \\
0x08 & W\_UPDATE\_CTRL & RW & 0 & [0] START; [1] ABORT; [2] VERIFY\_EN; [3] HALT\_ON\_ERR \\
0x0C & W\_UPDATE\_STATUS & RO & 0 & [0] BUSY; [1] DONE; [2] ERROR; [3] VERIFIED \\
